\displaystyle P_{\ell}+\sum_{j=1}^{{c_{\ell}^{r}}-1}p_{j}(1-x_{j})+p_{{c_{\ell}^{r}}}\frac{1-b_{\ell}-\sum_{j=1}^{{c_{\ell}^{r}}-1}B_{j}(1-x_{j})}{B_{{c_{\ell}^{r}}}}\leq M,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall\ \ell\in\{1,2,\cdots,1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{{c_{\ell}^{r}}-1}B_{j}(1-x_{j})\leq 1-b_{\ell},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall\ \ \ell\in\{1,2,\cdots,1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\}

\displaystyle B_{{c_{\ell}^{r}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{{c_{\ell}^{r}}-1}B_{j}(1-x_{j})\geq 1-b_{\ell},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall\ \ \ell\in\{1,2,\cdots,1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\}

\displaystyle x_{j}=x_{j}^{*},\hskip 5.69054ptj\in I\setminus\bar{I}

\displaystyle x_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 5.69054ptj\in\bar{I}

We have the following simple observation.

Observation 3
.

Let
M^{*}
and
{OPT}_{Bi}^{r}
be the optimal objective values of
\textbf{LP}_{\text{Bi-IP}}
and
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
, respectively, then
M^{*}\leq{OPT}_{Bi}^{r}
.

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r}
be an optimal solution to
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
. The observation follows directly as
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r}
together with
{OPT}_{Bi}^{r}
form a feasible solution to
\textbf{LP}_{\text{Bi-IP}}
.

In the meantime, we also have the following observation.

Observation 4
.

Let
\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex},M^{*}\}
be an extreme point optimal solution to
LP
{}_{\text{Bi-IP}}
, then
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex}
is also a feasible solution to
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
whose objective value is at most
M^{*}
.

The observation follows since by the definition of critical, (
4a
) is the largest profit the follower can achieve. Hence, when
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex}
in
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
, the objective value is bounded by
M^{*}
. Given the two observations above, we know
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex}
is an optimal solution to
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
and we have
M^{*}={OPT}_{Bi}^{r}
.
Finally, a near-optimal solution to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
can be obtained through the optimal solution to
\textbf{LP}_{\text{Bi-IP}}
, as implied by the following lemma.

Lemma 6
.

Let
\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex},M^{*}\}
be an extreme point optimal solution to
LP
{}_{\text{Bi-IP}}
. Define
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
such that
\tilde{x}_{j}=1
if
x_{j}^{ex}=1
, and
\tilde{x}_{j}=0
otherwise. Then
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
is a feasible solution of
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
with an objective value of at most
OPT+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
, where
OPT
is the optimal objective value of
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
.

Proof.

The feasibility of
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
is straightforward since

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}_{j}\tilde{x}_{j}=\sum_{j\in I\backslash\bar{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}_{j}{x}^{*}_{j}+\sum_{j\in\bar{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}_{j}\tilde{x}_{j}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}^{\prime}+\sum_{j\in\bar{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}_{j}x^{ex}_{j}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}^{\prime}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}^{\prime}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}.

Notice that
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
is also feasible for
Bi-IP
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
and
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
. Let
Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
,
{Obj}_{Bi}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
and
{Obj}_{Bi}^{r}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
be the objective values of
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
,
Bi-IP
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
and
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
by taking
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
, respectively. Since in
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
, the leader is facing a stronger follower who can select fractional items in
\bar{I}
, it follows that
{Obj}_{Bi}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq{Obj}_{Bi}^{r}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
.

Now we compare the objective values of two solutions to
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex}
and
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
. It is easy to see that in
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex}
, there are at most
(s_{A}+\frac{3(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon})
variables taking fractional values, and all these variables are in
\{x_{j}^{ex}:j\in\bar{I}\}
. So the leader in
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex}
selects at most
(s_{A}+\frac{3(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon})
more items in
\bar{I}
, compared with the leader in
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
. Consequently, the follower in
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex}
may select at most
(s_{A}+\frac{3(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon})
less items in
\bar{I}
, compared with the follower in
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
. Given that the objective value of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{ex}
is
{OPT}_{Bi}^{r}=M^{*}
, we have that

{Obj}_{Bi}^{r}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq{OPT}_{Bi}^{r}+(s_{A}+\frac{3(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon})\delta.

According to Lemma
4
and Lemma
5
, we have
Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq{Obj}_{Bi}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})+\epsilon
and
{OPT}_{Bi}^{r}\leq{OPT}_{Bi}+\delta
. In conclusion, we have

Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq{OPT}_{Bi}+(s_{A}+1+\frac{3(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon})\delta+\epsilon.

Furthermore,
{OPT}_{Bi}\leq OPT
by Lemma
4
. By setting
\delta=\epsilon^{2}
, Lemma
6
is proved.
∎

Hence, if
OPT\leq 1
, then a feasible solution with objective value of at most
OPT+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\leq 1+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
is found, thus Lemma
1
is proved, and Theorem
2
follows.

4
Approximation algorithm for IPC where
s_{B}
and
s_{A}
are constant

In this section, we prove our main result – Theorem
3
.

Theorem 3
.

When
s_{B}
and
s_{A}
are fixed constants, for an arbitrarily small number
\epsilon>0
, there exists an
(s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))
-approximation polynomial time algorithm for IPC.

Similar to the special case, by scaling item profits it suffices to show the following:

Lemma 7
.

Let
OPT
be the optimal objective value of
IPC
(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
. If
OPT\leq 1
, then for an arbitrarily small number
\epsilon>0
, there exists a polynomial time algorithm that returns a feasible solution to
\textbf{IPC}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
.

By further scaling the cost vectors and weight vectors, it suffices to find a near-optimal solution for
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
.

Major technical challenge.
Recall that the key to solving IPC for the special case of
s_{B}=1
is the establishment of
Bi-IP
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
, which is essentially equivalent to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
.
Bi-IP
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
is built upon the observation that the follower admits only
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
dominant choices on large items, where each dominant choice corresponds to the minimal budget needed by the follower to ensure a profit of
[(k-1)\epsilon,k\epsilon)
where
k\in\{1,2,\cdots,1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\}
. Because the number of follower’s choices on large items is small, its relaxation
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
has a small number of constraints (see (
3e
)), and therefore we can further transform
Bi-IP
{}_{r}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},1)
to
\textbf{LP}_{\text{Bi-IP}}
with a small number of constraints whose extreme point solution promises a good rounding. We aim to follow a similar method, however, when
s_{B}\geq 2
, we can no longer bound the follower’s choices on large items. This is because to achieve a profit of
[(k-1)\epsilon,k\epsilon)
where
k\in\{1,2,\cdots,1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\}
, the follower may have a huge number of different choices utilizing different budgets, where the budgets are now vectors instead of numbers, and are thus incomparable. To handle the problem, we use the idea of rounding: let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}=({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}[1],{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}[2],\cdots,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}[s_{B}])
and
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[1],\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}[2],\cdots,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}[s_{B}])
. We call the first dimension (
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}[1]
and
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[1]
’s) the principal dimension. The principal dimension will be treated the same as the special case and will
not
be rounded. The coordinates of other dimensions (
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}[h]
and
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[h]
’s for
2\leq h\leq s_{B}
) will be rounded. Then, we will be able to compare follower’s different choices on large items: if there are two choices both achieving profit within
[(k-1)\epsilon,k\epsilon)
for the same
k\in{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
, and furthermore, the summation of their weight vectors share the same rounded value in each dimension
h\in[2,s_{B}]
, then the choice with smaller value in the principal dimension of the summed weight vectors dominates the other choice. By doing so, our argument for the special case can be carried over to the principal dimension.

There is one problem with the idea of rounding above, that is, if we round up weight vectors and meanwhile enlarge the follower’s budget vector in dimension
h\in[2,s_{B}]
(to accommodate the rounding up), the optimal objective value of IPC may increase. However, we are able to show that the optimal objective value only increases by a factor of
s_{B}
(see Lemma
10
). This explains our approximation ratio of
s_{B}+\epsilon
.

Below we give a very brief walk through and the reader is referred to Appendix
A
for details.

Step 1
. We pick a small parameter
\delta
as the rounding precision, keep the coordinates of weight vectors on principal dimension intact, and round the coordinates on other dimensions. We also round the profits. By doing so we obtain a rounded instance
\tilde{I}_{\delta}
. Then we pick another small parameter
\tau
and enlarge the weight budget on dimension
h\in[2,s_{B}]
by a factor of
1+\tau
. By doing so we obtain:

\displaystyle\textbf{IPC}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}):\min\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84526pt{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

where
\displaystyle\bf y
solves the following:

\displaystyle\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\quad\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}

\displaystyle s.t.\quad\hskip 2.84526pt\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1+\tau,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall 2\leq i\leq s_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

where
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}}[1]
,
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[h]
,
2\leq h\leq s_{B}
and
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}
are rounded weights and profits. We are able to prove the following lemma which ensures that solving
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
gives a good approximate solution to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
:

Lemma 8
.

Let
0<\tau\leq 1/2
. Let
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
be any feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Then
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
is a feasible solution to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Let
\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
and
Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
be the objective values of
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
, respectively. If
2\delta\leq\tau\leq 1/2
, we have

Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq(1+\delta)\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq s_{B}(1+\delta)Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}).

Furthermore, let
\widetilde{OPT}_{\tau}
and
OPT
be the optimal objective values of
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, respectively. We have

OPT\leq(1+\delta)\widetilde{OPT}_{\tau}\leq s_{B}(1+\delta)OPT.

Step 2
. We handle large items. We first classify item profits into large, medium and small. We then classify item weights into large and small based on the largest coordinate in the weight vector, i.e.,
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}\|_{\infty}
. We say an item is large if it has a large weight or a large profit, and small otherwise. Let
S^{*}
be the leader’s optimal solution in
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Using a similar argument as the special case, we can prove the following.

Lemma 9
.

With
{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
additive error, we can guess out all items in
{S}^{*}
that have a large weight or a large profit by
n^{\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}/\delta^{s_{B}+1})}
enumerations.

Utilizing the fact that coordinates in dimension
h\in[2,s_{B}]
are all rounded, we can show that the follower only has a small number (i.e.
\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(s_{B}/\epsilon^{s_{B}})
) of dominant choices on large items, denoted as
\Theta
. By restricting the follower’s choices to
\Theta
, we can obtain a new bilevel integer programming
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Similar to
Bi-IP
({I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
in the special case,
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
has a small number of constraints.

Step 3
. We handle small items. Since
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
has a small number of constraints, we remove the integral constraint to obtain a relaxation
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Next, we transform this bilevel LP
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
to a standard (single level) LP, denoted as
\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}
. Note that here the transformation is much more complicated than that in the special case: in the special case we know that if the follower can choose items fractionally, then its optimal fractional solution is always obtained greedily with respect to the ratio (i.e., profit to weight), whereas it suffices to guess one single critical item. In the general case, if the follower can choose items fractionally, we can only guarantee that among all items whose rounded weight vector are the same except for the principal dimension (i.e.,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[h]
’s have the same rounded value for every
2\leq h\leq s_{B}
), the follower selects items greedily with respect to the principal ratio (i.e., profit to weight coordinate in the principal dimension). Therefore, we need to guess a subset of critical items, and the subscript
\lambda
in
\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}
corresponds to a set of parameters characterizing the subset of critical items. The most technical part is to show that the optimal solution to
\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}
gives a good approximation to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
(see Lemma
15
), where we need to create a sequence of ”intermediate” LPs. Finally, we obtain an extreme point solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
by solving
\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}
, and round it to an integral solution. The rounding error can be bounded due to that
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
contains a small number of constraints.

5
Approximation algorithm for IPC where
s_{B}
and
s_{A}
are arbitrary

We consider the most general setting of IPC where
s_{A}
and
s_{B}
are arbitrary (not necessarily polynomial in the input size).

We define
\max\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\}
as the
follower’s problem
. A separation oracle for the
leader’s problem
is an oracle such that given any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}\in[0,1]^{n}
, it either asserts that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}\in\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in[0,1]^{n}\}
, or returns a violating constraint. The goal of this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 4
.

Given a separation oracle
O_{L}
for the leader’s problem, and an oracle
O_{F}
for the follower’s problem that returns a
\rho
-approximation solution, there exists a
(\frac{\rho}{1-\alpha},\frac{1}{\alpha})
-bicriteria approximation algorithm for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
that returns a solution
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
such that
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}
, and

\max\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\}\leq\frac{\rho T^{*}}{1-\alpha},

where
T^{*}
is the optimal objective value of
IPC
(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
. Furthermore, the algorithm runs in polynomial oracle time.

We omit the proof of Theorem
4
here, and refer the reader to Appendix
B
.

6
Conclusions

In this paper, we consider a general two-player zero-sum Stackelberg game in which the leader interdicts some items to minimize the total profit that the follower could obtain from the remaining items. We obtain an
(s_{B}+\epsilon)
-approximation algorithm when
s_{A}
and
s_{B}
are both constant, and show that there does not exist any
(4/3-\epsilon)
-approximation algorithm when
s_{B}\geq 2
. Our algorithm is the best possible when
s_{B}=1
, however, it is not clear whether it is the best possible when
s_{B}
is larger than or equal to
2
. In particular, it is not clear whether an approximation algorithm with a ratio independent of
s_{B}
can be obtained. Furthermore, can we hope for a PTAS if
s_{B}\geq 2
but the constraints of the leader or the follower are not given by general inequalities but follow from common optimization problems? For example, what if the follower’s optimization problem is a bin packing problem? It would be interesting to investigate the bilevel generalization of well-known optimization problems, e.g., scheduling and bin packing.

Appendix A
Omitted contents in Section
4

In this section, we aim to prove our main result, Theorem
3
.

Theorem
3
.

When
s_{B}
and
s_{A}
are fixed constants, for an arbitrarily small number
\epsilon>0
, there exists an
(s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))
-approximation polynomial time algorithm for IPC.

The rest of this section is dedicated to proving the following Lemma
7
, which implies Theorem
3
by scaling item profits.

Lemma
7
.

Let
OPT
be the optimal objective value of
IPC
(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
. If
OPT\leq 1
, then for an arbitrarily small number
\epsilon>0
, there exists a polynomial time algorithm that returns a feasible solution to
\textbf{IPC}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
.

Throughout the proof of Lemma
7
, we consider the
IPC
(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
whose optimal objective value is at most 1. Since the case of
s_{B}=1
has been considered in Section
3
, in the following, we assume that
s_{B}\geq 2
.

A.1
Preprocessing

From now on, we assume
OPT\leq 1
. Without loss of generality, we further assume that
\max_{j}p_{j}\leq 1
.

Scaling.

We scale the matrix
\textstyle\bf A
and
\textstyle\bf B
such that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
. From now on we denote this IPC instance as
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Without loss of generality, we further assume that
\max_{j}\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
.

A.1.1
Generating a well-structured rounded item instance .

We aim to replace the original instance
I
with a well-structured rounded instance
\tilde{I}_{\delta}
, where
\delta
is the rounding precision.

Before giving our rounding scheme, we first present the augment lemma (i.e., Lemma
10
) which measures the loss incurred by slightly increasing the follower’s budget.

Augment lemma

We pick a small parameter
\tau>0
and enlarge the weight budget on dimension
h\in[2,s_{B}]
by a factor of
1+\tau
. By doing so we obtain:

\displaystyle\textbf{IPC}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}):\min\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle\bf p
\displaystyle\bf y

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84526pt{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

where
\displaystyle\bf y
solves the following:

\displaystyle\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\quad{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1+\tau,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall 2\leq i\leq s_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

That is,
IPC
{}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
denotes the instance where the budget vector of the follower is augmented such that value in its first dimension is still
1
, but values in its other dimensions are increased to
1+\tau
. We are interested in to what extent the optimal objective value may change when the budget of the follower is augmented as such. Note that any feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
is also a feasible solution to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, and vice versa. We have the following lemma.

Lemma 10

(Augment Lemma)
.

Let
0<\tau\leq 1/2
. Let
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
be any feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Then
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
is a feasible solution to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Let
Obj_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
and
Obj(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
be the objective values of
IPC
{}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
, respectively. We have

Obj_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq s_{B}Obj(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}).

Furthermore, let
OPT_{\tau}
and
OPT
be the optimal objective values of
IPC
{}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, respectively, then we have

OPT_{\tau}\leq s_{B}OPT.

Proof.

Let
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
be a feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Note that by taking
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
, the objective value of
IPC
{}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, denote by
Obj_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
, is exactly the optimal objective value of the following integer program:

\displaystyle\textbf{IP}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\quad

\displaystyle\bf p
\displaystyle\bf y

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1+\tau,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall 2\leq i\leq s_{B}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

Now we consider an optimal solution to
IP
{}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
and let it be
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\in\{0,1\}^{n}
. Let
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))
be the set of items selected by the follower in
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
, then the total weight vector of items in
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))
is at most
(1,1+\tau,\cdots,1+\tau)
. We decompose
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))
into
s_{B}
subsets
S_{1},S_{2},\cdots,S_{s_{B}}
as follows.

Initially, set
D_{0}=\{1\}
and we call dimension 1 the satisfied dimension. We first construct
S_{1}
in iteration 1. Consider each item
j\in S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))
and its weight vector
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}
. We remove the
1
-st coordinate of
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}
and obtain the projection of vector
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}
onto dimensions 2 to
s_{B}
, and denote it as
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}^{1}
. If there exists any item
j
such that
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}^{1}\|_{\infty}\geq\tau
, then
S_{1}
consists of this single item. Otherwise,
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}^{1}\|_{\infty}<\tau
for all items in
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))
. Then
S_{1}
consists of some
\ell_{1}
items
j^{1}_{1},j^{1}_{2},\cdots,j^{1}_{\ell_{1}}
in
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))
such that

\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sum$}}_{k=1}^{\ell_{1}-1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j^{1}_{k}}^{1}\|_{\infty}<\tau\leq\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sum$}}_{k=1}^{\ell_{1}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j^{1}_{k}}^{1}\|_{\infty}.

For each dimension
2\leq i\leq s_{B}
, if
\sum_{j\in S_{1}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[i]\geq\tau
, then we say dimension
i
is satisfied (in iteration 1). Let
D_{1}\supset D_{0}
be the set of all satisfied dimensions so far.

Suppose we have constructed
S_{1},\cdots,S_{h}
, and
D_{h}
is the set of all satisfied dimensions so far. We now construct
S_{h+1}
in iteration
h+1
. Let
S_{[1:h]}=\cup_{\iota=1}^{h}S_{\iota}
. Consider each item
j\in S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))\backslash S_{[1:h]}
and its weight vector
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}
. We remove the
i
-th coordinate of
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}
if
i\in D_{h}
. By doing so we obtain the projection of vector
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}
onto all unsatisfied dimensions and denote it as
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}^{h+1}
. If there exists any item
j\in S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))\backslash S_{[1:h]}
such that
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}^{h+1}\|_{\infty}\geq\tau
, then
S_{h+1}
consists of this single item. Otherwise,
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}^{h+1}\|_{\infty}<\tau
for all items in
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))\backslash S_{[1:h]}
. Then
S_{h+1}
consists of some
\ell_{h+1}
items
j^{h+1}_{1},j^{h+1}_{2},\cdots,j^{h+1}_{\ell_{h+1}}
in
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))\backslash S_{[1:h]}
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sum$}}_{k=1}^{\ell_{h+1}-1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j^{h+1}_{k}}^{h+1}\|_{\infty}<\tau\leq\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sum$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sum$}}_{k=1}^{\ell_{h+1}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j^{h+1}_{k}}^{h+1}\|_{\infty}.

(8)

For each dimension
i\not\in D_{h}
, if
\sum_{j\in S_{h+1}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[i]\geq\tau
, then we say dimension
i
is satisfied (in iteration
h+1
). Let
D_{h+1}\supset D_{h}
be the set of all satisfied dimensions so far.

Notice that in each iteration, at least one more dimension becomes satisfied, hence the above procedure stops after at most
s_{B}-1
iterations and produces
S_{1},S_{2},\cdots,S_{s_{B}-1}
(if it stops at iteration
h<s_{B}-1
, we may simply let
S_{h+1}
to
S_{s_{B}-1}
be
\emptyset
). Define
S_{s_{B}}=S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))\setminus(\cup_{\iota=1}^{s_{B}-1}S_{\iota})
.

We claim that, every
S_{h}
induces a feasible solution to the following
IP
(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))
(by setting
y_{j}=1
if and only if
j\in S_{h}
,
y_{j}=0
otherwise):

\displaystyle\textbf{IP}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\quad

\displaystyle\bf p
\displaystyle\bf y

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

To see the claim, recall the construction of each
S_{h+1}(h\geq 0)
. If
S_{h+1}
only contains a single item, then since each item
j
satisfies that
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
,
S_{h+1}
obviously gives a feasible solution to
IP
(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
. Otherwise,
S_{h+1}
contains 2 or more items. It is easy to see that
\sum_{j\in S_{[1:h]}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[1]\leq 1
. For every
k\in D_{h}\setminus\{1\}
, we know that
\sum_{j\in S_{[1:h]}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[k]\geq\tau
, and consequently

\sum_{j\in S_{h+1}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[k]\leq\sum_{j\in S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))\setminus S_{[1:h]}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[k]\leq 1,\quad\forall k\in D_{h}\setminus\{1\}.

Meanwhile, by the way we construct
S_{h+1}
,
S_{h+1}
can contain more than one item only if
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}^{h}\|_{\infty}<\tau
for any
j\in S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}))\setminus S_{[1:h]}
, hence, by Eq (
8
), we know that

\sum_{j\in S_{h+1}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[k]\leq 2\tau\leq 1,\quad\forall k\not\in D_{h}.

The last inequality holds by the fact that
0<\tau\leq 1/2
. Consequently,
\sum_{j\in S_{h+1}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
, implying that
S_{h+1}
always induces a feasible solution to
IP
(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
.

Notice that the optimal objective value of
IP
(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
is
Obj(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
, whereas the objective value of the solution induced by
S_{h+1}
is at most
Obj(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
, implying that the solution induced by
\cup_{h=1}^{s_{B}}S_{h}
is at most
s_{B}Obj(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
, that is
Obj_{\tau}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq s_{B}Obj(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
. Particularly, given an optimal solution
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
of
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, we have
Obj_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*})\leq s_{B}OPT
. Since the optimal solution of
IPC
{}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
may achieve an even smaller objective value, it follows that
OPT_{\tau}\leq s_{B}OPT
. Hence, Lemma
10
is proved.∎

Rounding Scheme

Now, we are ready to generate a well-structured rounded instance. Let
\delta>0
be some small parameter to be fixed later (in particular, we can choose
\delta=\epsilon^{2s_{B}+4}
). We say an item
j\in I
has a large weight if
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}\|_{\infty}>\delta
, and a small weight otherwise.

1. Rounding up the weight vectors.
For each large-weight item
j\in I
, consider its weight vector
