\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[1],\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[2],\dots,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[s_{B}])
. We keep
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[1]
unchanged and round up the coordinate
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[i]

(2\leq i\leq s_{B})
to the nearest value of the form
\frac{\delta^{2}}{s_{B}}(1+\delta)^{h}
. More precisely, for each
2\leq i\leq s_{B}
, if
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[i]\leq\frac{\delta^{2}}{s_{B}}
, we round it up to
\frac{\delta^{2}}{s_{B}}
; otherwise for some integer
h\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\delta)
, it holds that
\frac{\delta^{2}}{s_{B}}(1+\delta)^{h}<\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[i]\leq\frac{\delta^{2}}{s_{B}}(1+\delta)^{h+1}
, and we round it up to
\frac{\delta^{2}}{s_{B}}(1+\delta)^{h+1}
. For small-weight items, we keep their weight vectors unchanged. Let
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}(1\leq j\leq n)
be the rounded weight vector.

2. Rounding down the profits.
For each item
j\in I
, consider its profit
p_{j}
. If
p_{j}\leq\delta^{2}
, we keep it as it is; otherwise
p_{j}>\delta^{2}
, we round the profit down to the largest value of the form
\delta^{2}(1+\delta)^{h}
. For profits whose values are at least
\delta^{2}
, simple calculation shows there are at most
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\delta)
distinct rounded profits. Denote by
\tilde{p}_{j}(1\leq j\leq n)
the rounded profit, we have
\tilde{p}_{j}\leq p_{j}\leq(1+\delta)\tilde{p}_{j}
. Let

\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}=(\tilde{p}_{1},\tilde{p}_{2},\cdots,\tilde{p}_{n})
.

3. Generating
I
with a well-structured instance.
Let
\tilde{I}_{\delta}=\{1,2,\cdots,n\}
denote the
rounded
instance where each item
j\in\tilde{I}_{\delta}
is associated with a
rounded
weight vector
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}
, a
rounded
profit
\tilde{p}_{j}
and a cost vector
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}_{j}
.

We replace
I
with
\tilde{I}_{\delta}
in
IPC
{}_{\tau}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. By doing so we obtain:

\displaystyle\textbf{IPC}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}):\min\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84526pt{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

where
\displaystyle\bf y
solves the following:

\displaystyle\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\quad\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}

\displaystyle s.t.\quad\hskip 2.84526pt\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1

(9a)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1+\tau,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall 2\leq i\leq s_{B}

(9b)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

Since we do not change the cost vectors of items, any feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
is also a feasible solution to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, and vice versa. Furthermore, we have the following Lemma
8
which ensures that solving
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
gives a good approximation solution to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
.

Lemma
8
.

Let
0<\tau\leq 1/2
. Let
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
be any feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Then
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
is a feasible solution to
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Let
\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
and
Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
be the objective values of
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
, respectively. If
\tau\geq 2\delta
, we have

Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq(1+\delta)\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq s_{B}(1+\delta)Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}).

Furthermore, let
\widetilde{OPT}_{\tau}
and
OPT
be the optimal objective values of
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, respectively. We have

OPT\leq(1+\delta)\widetilde{OPT}_{\tau}\leq s_{B}(1+\delta)OPT.

Towards the proof of Lemma
8
,
we have the following observation.

Observation 5
.

Given
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in[0,1]^{n}
satisfying
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
, we have

•

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1
;

•

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1+2\delta,\hskip 5.69054pti=2,3,\cdots,s_{B}
.

Proof.

Given that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in[0,1]^{n}
satisfies
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
, then there are at most
s_{B}/\delta
large items in
I
picked in
\textstyle\bf y
. According to the rounding above, we have

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1
;

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq(1+\delta)\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}+\frac{\delta^{2}}{s_{B}}\cdot\frac{s_{B}}{\delta}\leq 1+2\delta,\hskip 5.69054pti=2,3,\cdots,s_{B}
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
8
.

Let
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
be any feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Denote by
\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
and
Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
the objective values of
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
IPC
(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
, respectively. Note that
\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
is exactly the optimal objective value of the following integer program:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textbf{IP}}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\quad

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1+\tau,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall 2\leq i\leq s_{B}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

Meanwhile,
Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
is the optimal objective value of the following integer program:

\displaystyle\textbf{IP}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\quad

\displaystyle\bf p
\displaystyle\bf y

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall 1\leq i\leq s_{B}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

We claim that
Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq(1+\delta)\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
. Towards this, let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
be an optimal solution of
\textbf{IP}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
. By the definition of
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}
, we have
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq(1+\delta)\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
. It suffices to show that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
is a feasible solution to
\widetilde{\textbf{IP}}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
, which is guaranteed by Observation
5
and the fact that
\tau\geq 2\delta
.

Let
OPT
and
\widetilde{OPT}_{\tau}
be the optimal objective values of
\textbf{IPC}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
{\textbf{IPC}}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, respectively, then we have
OPT\leq(1+\delta)\widetilde{OPT}_{\tau}
. This is because that the optimal solution to
{\textbf{IPC}}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
is also a feasible solution to
\textbf{IPC}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
while the optimal solution to
\textbf{IPC}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
may achieve an even smaller value.

It remains to prove that
\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq s_{B}{Obj}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
and
\widetilde{OPT}_{\tau}\leq s_{B}OPT
. Towards this, we introduce a new bilevel program
{\textbf{IPC}}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, which is constructed by replacing (
9b
) in
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1(\forall 2\leq i\leq s_{B}
). That is, we reduce the budget of the follower to
\textstyle\bf 1
. Let
\widetilde{OPT}
be the optimal objective value of
{\textbf{IPC}}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and let
\widetilde{Obj}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
be the objective value of
{\textbf{IPC}}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
.

Compare
{\textbf{IPC}}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
{\textbf{IPC}}({I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. We see that in this two bilevel programs, the budgets of the leader and follower are the same, while the weight vectors in
\tilde{I}_{\delta}
is always larger and the profits in
\tilde{I}_{\delta}
is always smaller. Hence, for the same leader’s solution, the follower in
{\textbf{IPC}}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
achieves a smaller objective, thus
\widetilde{Obj}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
. Furthermore, we have
\widetilde{OPT}\leq OPT
. This is that because that the optimal solution to
{\textbf{IPC}}(I,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
is also a feasible solution to
\textbf{IPC}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
while the optimal solution to
\textbf{IPC}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
may achieve an even smaller value.

Compare
{\textbf{IPC}}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. According to Lemma
10
, we have
\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq s_{B}\widetilde{Obj}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
and
\widetilde{OPT}_{\tau}\leq s_{B}\widetilde{OPT}
.

To summarize, we know
Obj(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq(1+\delta)\widetilde{Obj}_{\tau}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq s_{B}(1+\delta)\widetilde{Obj}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq s_{B}(1+\delta){Obj}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
and
OPT\leq(1+\delta)\widetilde{OPT}_{\tau}\leq s_{B}(1+\delta)\widetilde{OPT}\leq s_{B}(1+\delta)OPT
. Lemma
8
is proved.
∎

In the rest, we focus on
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
which is obtained by setting
\tau=2\delta
in
IPC
{}_{\tau}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Assuming
OPT\leq 1
, we aim to design a polynomial time algorithm which returns a feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
. According to Lemma
8
, this solution is exactly the solution that Lemma
7
requires, and Theorem
3
is proved.

A.1.2
Item Classification of
\tilde{I}_{\delta}

Recall that every item
j\in\tilde{I}_{\delta}
is associated with a profit
\tilde{p}_{j}
and a weight vector
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}
.

Classifying Profits:
We say an item
j\in\tilde{I}_{\delta}
has a large profit if
\tilde{p}_{j}>\delta
; a medium profit if
\delta^{2}<\tilde{p}_{j}\leq\delta
; and a small profit if
\tilde{p}_{j}\leq\delta^{2}
.

Classifying Weights:

We say an item
j\in\tilde{I}_{\delta}
has a large weight if
\|\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}\|_{\infty}>\delta
; and a small weight otherwise.

We say an item in
\tilde{I}_{\delta}
is
large
(for the follower) if it has a large-profit or a large-weight. Otherwise, the item is
small
. To find a desired approximation solution to
\textbf{IPC}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I}_{\delta},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
in polynomial time
, we will handle large items and small items separately. For simplicity, from now on, we use
\tilde{I}
to denote
\tilde{I}_{\delta}
.

A.2
Handling Large Items

A.2.1
Determining the leader’s choice on large items

Instead of considering
\textbf{IPC}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
directly, we introduce a bilevel program
IPC
{}_{\overline{2\delta}}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, which is obtained by replacing (
9b
) in
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1+\overline{2\delta}(\forall 2\leq i\leq s_{B}
), where
\overline{2\delta}=2\delta+\epsilon(1+2\delta)
. It is easy to see that for fixed
\textstyle\bf x
, the objective value of
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
is smaller than that of
IPC
{}_{\overline{2\delta}}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Let
\widetilde{OPT}_{\overline{2\delta}}
be the optimal objective value of
IPC
{}_{\overline{2\delta}}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Recall that
OPT\leq 1
, according to Lemma
8
, we have
\widetilde{OPT}_{\overline{2\delta}}\leq s_{B}OPT\leq s_{B}
.

Denote by
{S}^{*}
the items selected by the leader in an optimal solution of
IPC
{}_{\overline{2\delta}}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. The goal of this section is to guess
large items
in
{S}^{*}
in polynomial time.

Large-profit small-weight items.
Notice that if there are at least
s_{B}/\delta
such items in
\tilde{I}\setminus S^{*}
for the follower to select, then selecting any
s_{B}/\delta
of them gives a solution to
IPC
{}_{\overline{2\delta}}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with an objective value strictly larger than
s_{B}
, violating the fact that
{\widetilde{OPT}}_{\overline{2\delta}}\leq s_{B}
.
Thus
S^{*}
must include all except at most
s_{B}/\delta-1
such items, which can be guessed out via
n^{{\mathcal{O}}({s_{B}/\delta})}
enumerations. Hence, we have the following observation.

Observation 6
.

With
n^{{\mathcal{O}}({s_{B}/\delta})}
enumerations, we can guess out all large-profit small-weight items in
{S}^{*}
.

Large-weight small-profit items.
Notice that the follower can select at most
\frac{s_{B}(1+\overline{2\delta})}{\delta}
items from this subgroup and their total profit is at most
\delta^{2}\cdot\frac{s_{B}(1+\overline{2\delta})}{\delta}=s_{B}(1+\overline{2\delta})\delta
. Hence, even if the leader does not select any such item, the objective value can increase by at most
{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
, which leads to the following observation.

Observation 7
.

With an additive error of
{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
, we may assume that
{S}^{*}
does not contain large-weight small-profit items.

Large-weight large/medium-profit items.
Notice that the follower can select at most
\frac{s_{B}(1+\overline{2\delta})}{\delta}
items from this group. Recall the weight vector
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}
of a large weight item. For every
2\leq i\leq s_{B}
,
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]
may take at most
\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(1/\delta)
distinct values. Furthermore, large/medium profits can also take
\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(1/\delta)
distinct values. Hence, we can divide large/medium-profit large-weight items into
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\delta^{s_{B}})
subgroups, such that items in the same subgroup have the same profit and the same rounded weight vector (except dimension 1), i.e., if items
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
belong to the same subgroup, then
\tilde{p}_{j_{1}}=\tilde{p}_{j_{2}}
, and
(\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j_{1}}[2],\cdots,\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j_{1}}[s_{B}])=(\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j_{2}}[2],\cdots,\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j_{2}}[s_{B}])
. Let
\{S_{h}:h\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\delta^{s_{B}})\}
be the set of these subgroups. If there are two items in
S_{h}
which are not selected by the leader, then the follower always prefers the item whose weight vector has a smaller value in the first dimension. Hence we have the following lemma.

Lemma 11
.

With
n^{\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}/\delta^{s_{B}+1})}
enumerations, we can guess out all large-weight large/medium-profit items in
{S}^{*}
.

Proof.

We can apply the same argument as the proof of Lemma
2
. That is, for each subgroup
S_{h}
,
S^{*}\cap S_{h}
can be determined through guessing out the following
\frac{s_{B}(1+\overline{2\delta})}{\delta}
key items in
S_{h}
: among items in
S_{h}\setminus S^{*}
, which is the item whose weight vector has the
k
-th smallest value in the first dimension for
k=1,2,\cdots,\frac{s_{B}(1+\overline{2\delta})}{\delta}
? Thus via
n^{{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}/\delta)}
enumerations, we can guess out all large-weight large/medium-profit items in
{S}^{*}\cap S_{h}
. Moreover, via total
n^{\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}/\delta^{s_{B}+1})}
enumerations, we can guess out all large-weight large/medium-profit items in
{S}^{*}
. ∎

To summarize, our above analysis leads to the following lemma:

Lemma
9
.

With
{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
additive error, we can guess out all items in
{S}^{*}
that have a large weight or a large profit by
n^{\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}/\delta^{s_{B}+1})}
enumerations.

Let
\hat{I}
be the set of small items, i.e., items of medium/small-profit and small-weight. Then
\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}
is the set of large items. Denote by
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
the optimal solution to
IPC
{}_{\overline{2\delta}}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, which is corresponding to
S^{*}
. Then
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
is a feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}
. This is because that
\widetilde{OPT}_{\overline{2\delta}}\leq s_{B}
, and the follower in
IPC
{}_{\overline{2\delta}}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
is stronger. In the following, we assume that large items in
S^{*}
have been correctly guessed. Hence, the values in
\{x_{j}^{*}:\forall j\in\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}\}
are known. We let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}^{\prime}
be the sum of the cost vectors of these guessed-out large items.

A.2.2
Finding the follower’s dominant choices on large items

Consider all the large items. Even if the leader’s choice on large items is fixed, the follower may still have exponentially many different choices on the remaining large items. The goal of this subsection is to show that, among these choices of the follower, it suffices to restrict our attention to a few ”dominant” choices that always outperform other choices.

For simplicity, we re-index items such that
\hat{I}=\{1,2,\cdots,\hat{n}\}
, then
\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}=\{\hat{n}+1,\cdots,n\}
, where
\hat{n}\leq n
. Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{2\delta}=(1,1+2\delta,\cdots,1+2\delta)
, which is the follower’s budget vector in
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Denote by
I^{\prime}
the set of items in
\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}
which are
not
selected by the leader. Note that we have correctly guessed out large items in
S^{*}
and the objective value of
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
is at most
s_{B}
, thus the follower cannot select items from
I^{\prime}
with total profit larger than
s_{B}
. Since
I^{\prime}
is a subset of large-profit or large-weight items, the follower can select at most
{\mathcal{O}}({s_{B}}/{\delta})
items from
I^{\prime}
.

Let
V
denote the largest integer satisfying
\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{V}\leq 1+2\delta
. For each integer
k\in[1,1+s_{B}/{\epsilon}]
and each
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}:=(v_{2},v_{3},\cdots,v_{s_{B}})
where
v_{i}\in\{-1,0,1,2,\cdots,V\}
, we define the following sub-problem: Among all follower’s choices on
I^{\prime}
, find out one whose summation of weight vectors has the smallest value in first dimension, and this choice must satisfy: (i) the summation of profits is within
[(k-1)\epsilon,k\epsilon)
; (ii) in each dimension
i\in[2,s_{B}]
, the
remaining
follower’s budget is within
[l_{v_{i}},r_{v_{i}})
, where
[l_{v_{i}},r_{v_{i}})=[0,\epsilon)
if
v_{i}=-1
, otherwise
[l_{v_{i}},r_{v_{i}}]=[\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{v_{i}},\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{v_{i}+1})
. The sub-problem is formulated as follows:

\displaystyle\textbf{SP}(k;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}):\min\limits_{y_{j}:j\in I^{\prime}}\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in I^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in I^{\prime}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}\in[(k-1)\epsilon,k\epsilon)

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in I^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1

\displaystyle 1+2\delta-\sum_{j\in I^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\in[l_{v_{i}},r_{v_{i}}),\indent i=2,3,\cdots,s_{B}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in\{0,1\},j\in I^{\prime}

Note that there are at most
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}({s_{B}}/{\epsilon^{s_{B}}})
distinct sub-problems. Denote by
\textbf{opt}(k;\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}})
an optimal solution of
\textbf{SP}(k;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})
, if it exists. Let
\Theta=\{\textbf{opt}(k;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})|k,v_{i}\in\mathbb{Z};k\in[1,\frac{\epsilon+s_{B}}{\epsilon}];v_{i}\in[-1,V]\text{ for }i=2,3,\cdots,s_{B}\}
, which contains the follower’s
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}({s_{B}}/{\epsilon^{s_{B}}})
possible choices on
I^{\prime}
. Recall that the follower can select at most
{\mathcal{O}}({s_{B}}/{\delta})
items from
I^{\prime}
. Thus via
n^{{\mathcal{O}}({s_{B}}/{\delta})}
enumerations, for each sub-problem
\textbf{SP}(k;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})
, we can return its optimal solution or assert there does not exist a feasible solution for this sub-problem, which means that
\Theta
can be determined in polynomial time. We will show that to select small items to accompany the large items in
S^{*}
, it suffices to consider the choices in
\Theta
.

For the
l
-th choice in
\Theta
, let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{l}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{2\delta}
and
P_{l}
be the summation of weight vectors and the summation of profits, respectively. Then the follower has a residual budget of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{2\delta}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{l}
. Assuming that the
l
-th choice in
\Theta
is
\textbf{opt}(k;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})
,
define
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}=(1-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{l}[1],r_{v_{2}},\cdots,r_{v_{s_{B}}})
, it follows that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{2\delta}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{l}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}
, moreover, we have the following observation.

Observation 8
.

Given the
l
-th choice in
\Theta
, say
\textbf{opt}(k,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})
, then any feasible solution
\{y_{j}:j\in I^{\prime}\}
of
SP
(k,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})
satisfies

•

\sum_{j\in I^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}[1]\leq 1
;

•

\sum_{j\in I^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}[i]\leq 1+\overline{2\delta},\hskip 5.69054pti=2,3,\cdots,s_{B}
,

where
\overline{2\delta}=2\delta+\epsilon(1+2\delta)
.

Recall that the leader has a residual budget of
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}^{\prime}
for items in
\hat{I}
, and by guessing we already know the value of
x^{*}_{j}
for
j\in\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}
. Define
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]=(y_{1},y_{2},\cdots,y_{\hat{n}})
. Consider the following bilevel program:

\displaystyle\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}):\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\quad

\displaystyle P_{l}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}_{j}x_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}^{\prime}

(10a)

\displaystyle x_{j}=x_{j}^{*},\hskip 19.91692pt\forall j\in\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}

(10b)

\displaystyle x_{j}\in\{0,1\},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

(10c)

\displaystyle\text{where integer }l,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]\text{ solves the following:}

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq|\Theta|}\max_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad P_{l}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

(10d)

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pts.t.\hskip 5.69054pt\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}y_{j}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}

(10e)

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36967pty_{j}\leq 1-x_{j},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

(10f)

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36967pty_{j}\in\{0,1\},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

(10g)

We have the following lemma.

Lemma 12
.

Assume that
OPT\leq 1
. Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}
be any feasible solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, then
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}
is also a feasible solution to
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
.
Let
Obj^{MBi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})
and
\widetilde{Obj}_{2\delta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})
be the objective values of
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}
, respectively. We have

\widetilde{Obj}_{2\delta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})\leq Obj^{MBi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})+\epsilon.

Furthermore, let
OPT^{Mbi}
be the optimal objective value of
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. We have

OPT^{MBi}\leq s_{B}.

Proof.

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}
be any feasible solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, denote by
Obj^{MBi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})
and
\widetilde{Obj}_{2\delta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})
the objective values of
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}
, respectively. Note that
\widetilde{Obj}_{2\delta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})
is the optimal objective of the following program:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textbf{IP}}_{2\delta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\quad

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1+2\delta,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall 2\leq i\leq s_{B}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

Notice that
x^{\prime}_{j}=x_{j}^{*}(\forall j\in\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I})
and the objective value of
IPC
{}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
is at most
s_{B}
. Thus in
\widetilde{\textbf{IP}}_{2\delta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})
, the maximum profit the follower could achieve from
\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}
is at most
s_{B}
. Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime}
be an optimal solution to
\widetilde{\textbf{IP}}_{2\delta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})
, then there exists a choice
\textbf{opt}(k;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})\in\Theta
such that

•

\sum_{j\in{\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{\prime}_{j}\in[(k-1)\epsilon,k\epsilon)
;

•

1+2\delta-\sum_{j\in{\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y^{\prime}_{j}\in[l_{v_{i}},r_{v_{i}})
,
\quad i=2,3,\cdots,s_{B}.

Assuming that
\textbf{opt}(k;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})
is the
l
-th choice in
\Theta
, then it follows that

•

P_{l}\in[(k-1)\epsilon,k\epsilon)
;

•

\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y^{\prime}_{j}\leq 1-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{l}[1]={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}[1]
;

•

\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y^{\prime}_{j}\leq 1+2\delta-\sum_{j\in{\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y^{\prime}_{j}<r_{v_{i}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}[i]
,
\quad i=2,3,\cdots,s_{B}.

Thus
\sum_{j\in{\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{\prime}_{j}\leq P_{l}+\epsilon
, and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime}[\hat{I}]=(y^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,y^{\prime}_{\hat{n}})
is a feasible solution of the following program:

\displaystyle{\textbf{IP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}y_{j}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{\prime}_{j},\forall j\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in\{0,1\},\forall j\in\hat{I}

This means that
l
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime}[\hat{I}]
form a feasible solution to the program (
10d
) - (
10g
) when
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}
. Then we have

\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime}=\sum_{j\in{\tilde{I}\setminus}\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{\prime}_{j}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{\prime}_{j}\leq P_{l}+\epsilon+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{\prime}_{j}\leq Obj^{MBi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})+\epsilon.

Thus the first part of Lemma
12
is proved.

It remains to prove that the optimal objective value of
MBi-IP
(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
is at most
s_{B}
. Towards this, we compare
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
IPC
{}_{\overline{2\delta}}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Recall that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
and
\widetilde{OPT}_{\overline{2\delta}}
are the optimal solution and the optimal objective value of
IPC
{}_{\overline{2\delta}}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, respectively. Notice that
\widetilde{OPT}_{\overline{2\delta}}\leq s_{B}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
is feasible for
MBi-IP
(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, it suffices to prove that the objective value of
MBi-IP
(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
is no larger than
\widetilde{OPT}_{\overline{2\delta}}
.

Note that
\widetilde{OPT}_{\overline{2\delta}}
is the optimal objective value of the following program:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textbf{IP}}_{\overline{2\delta}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\quad
