\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[1]y_{j}\leq 1

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq 1+\overline{2\delta},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall 2\leq i\leq s_{B}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

Given
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
, there exist
l^{\prime}\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{l^{\prime}}[\hat{I}]:=(y^{l^{\prime}}_{1},y^{l^{\prime}}_{2},\cdots,y^{l^{\prime}}_{\hat{n}})
which form an optimal solution to the program (
10d
) - (
10g
) when
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
. Note that the objective value of
MBi-IP
(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
is

P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{l^{\prime}}_{j}
. Define
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
as follows:
y_{j}=y^{l^{\prime}}_{j}
if
j\in\hat{I}
;
y_{j}=1
if item
j
is selected in the
l^{\prime}
-th choice in
\Theta
;
y_{j}=0
otherwise. Then we have

\sum\limits_{j\in\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}+\sum\limits_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}_{l^{\prime}}[i]+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l^{\prime}}[i],\quad i=1,2,\cdots,s_{B}.

It follows that
\textstyle\bf y
is a feasible solution to
\widetilde{\textbf{IP}}_{\overline{2\delta}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*})
(by Observation
8
). Thus
P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{l^{\prime}}_{j}
is no larger than
\widetilde{OPT}_{\overline{2\delta}}
, and the second part of Lemma
12
is proved.∎

In the following, we focus on
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Assuming
OPT\leq 1
, our goal is to design a polynomial time algorithm which returns a feasible solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
. According to Lemma
12
and Lemma
8
, this solution is exactly the solution that Lemma
7
requires.

A.3
Handling Small Items

The goal of this section is to select
small items
to accompany the large items of
{S}^{*}
that have been guessed out in Section
A.2.1
. It suffices to solve
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Towards this, we first obtain a linear relaxation of
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
where both the leader and the follower can select items fractionally. To solve the relaxation, we construct a single level LP and find its extreme point optimal fractional solution (see Section
A.3.1
). Finally we round this fractional solution to a desired approximation solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
(see Section
A.3.2
).

A.3.1
Finding a fractional solution

Replace (
10c
) and (
10g
) in
MBi-IP
(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
x_{j}\in[0,1](\forall j\in\hat{I})
and
y_{j}\in[0,1](\forall j\in\hat{I})
, respectively, we obtain a relaxation of
MBi-IP
(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
as follows:

\displaystyle\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}):\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\quad

\displaystyle P_{l}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}_{j}x_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}^{\prime}

(11a)

\displaystyle x_{j}=x_{j}^{*},\hskip 19.91692pt\forall j\in\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}

(11b)

\displaystyle x_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 11.38109pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

(11c)

\displaystyle\text{where integer }l,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]\text{ solves the following:}

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq|\Theta|}\max_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad P_{l}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

(11d)

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pts.t.\hskip 5.69054pt\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}y_{j}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}

(11e)

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36967pty_{j}\leq 1-x_{j},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

(11f)

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36967pty_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 11.38109pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

(11g)

We have the following lemma.

Lemma 13
.

Assume that
OPT\leq 1
. Let
OPT^{MBi}_{r}
and
OPT^{MBi}
be the optimal objective values of
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, respectively. Then we have

OPT^{MBi}_{r}\leq OPT^{MBi}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta).

Proof.

It is not straightforward if we compare
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
directly, as both the follower and the leader become stronger in the relaxation (in the sense they can pack items fractionally). Towards this, we introduce an intermediate bilevel program
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{in}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, which is obtained by replacing (
11c
) in
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
x_{j}\in\{0,1\}(\forall j\in\hat{I})
, that is, we only allow the follower to select items fractionally but not the leader. Denote by
OPT^{MBi}_{in}
the optimal objective value of
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{in}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
.

First, we compare
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{in}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. We see that in
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
the follower is facing a stronger leader who is allowed to pack items fractionally, and it thus follows that
OPT^{MBi}_{r}\leq OPT^{MBi}_{in}
.

Next, we compare
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{in}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Note that the leader’s solution must be integral in both programs. Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi}
be an optimal solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, then
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi}
is also a feasible solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{in}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Once the leader fixes his solution as
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi}
in
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{in}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, there exist
l^{\prime}\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime}[\hat{I}]
which form an optimal solution to the program (
11d
) - (
11g
) when
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi}
. Then the objective value of
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{in}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi}
is
Obj^{MBi}_{in}=P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{\prime}_{j}.
We have the following two observations:

•

OPT^{MBi}_{in}\leq Obj^{MBi}_{in}
. This is because that

{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi}
is just a feasible solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{in}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, while the optimal solution of the leader may achieve an even smaller objective value.

•

Obj^{MBi}_{in}\leq OPT^{MBi}+s_{B}\delta
.

Towards the second observation, notice that
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{\prime}_{j}
is the optimal objective value of the following linear program:

\displaystyle{\textbf{LP}}(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}y_{j}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{MBi}_{j},\hskip 2.84526pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 17.07164pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime}[\hat{I}]
be an extreme point optimal fractional solution to
LP
(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi})
, we define
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]\in\{0,1\}^{\hat{n}}
as follows:
y_{j}=1
if
y^{\prime}_{j}=1
;
y_{j}=0
otherwise. Note that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]
is a feasible solution to the following integer program:

\displaystyle{\textbf{IP}}(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}y_{j}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{MBi}_{j},\hskip 2.84526pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in\{0,1\},\hskip 11.38109pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

Then
l^{\prime}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]
form a feasible solution to the program (
10d
) - (
10g
) when
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{MBi}
, thus we have
P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}\leq OPT^{MBi}
.
Notice that there are at most
s_{B}
variables in
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime}[\hat{I}]
taking fractional values and
\tilde{p}_{j}\leq\delta
for
j\in\hat{I}
, it thus follows that
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{\prime}_{j}\leq\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}+s_{B}\delta
.

To summarize, we have
OPT^{MBi}_{r}\leq OPT^{MBi}_{in}\leq Obj^{MBi}_{in}\leq OPT^{MBi}+s_{B}\delta\leq s_{B}+s_{B}\delta
, where the last inequality is guaranteed by Lemma
12
.∎

The rest of this section is to solve
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Since
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
is a bilevel linear program, which is hard to solve (Jeroslow
[
23
]
), we consider to find an approximation solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
. Towards this, in the following Section
A.3.1
- Section
A.3.1
, we transform
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
to a standard (single level) LP whose extreme point optimal solution is what we want. Moreover, both the construction of the single level program and the algorithm to solve the single level program run in polynomial time.

Preprocessing of small items

Scaling.
Recall that
\{\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}:j\in\hat{I}\}
is the set of weight vectors of small items in
\tilde{I}
. Given
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
, for each item
j\in\hat{I}
, we scale its weight vector
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}
by
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}
, and let
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}
denote the scaled weight vector. That is,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}[i]=\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}[i]/{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l}[i](\forall 1\leq i\leq s_{B})
.

It is easy to see that
\{y_{j}:\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}_{j}y_{j}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}_{l},\forall j\in\hat{I}\}
is equal to
\{y_{j}:\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\forall j\in\hat{I}\}
, thus we can replace (
11e
) in
MBi-IP
{}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
for all
j\in\hat{I}
.
4
4

4

If
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\|_{\infty}>1
, then item
j
can not be selected by the follower, thus
y_{j}=0
.

In the following we will round the scaled weight vectors of small items, however, we do
not
really change the input instance. We still keep the original weight vectors of items but only use the rounded weight vectors to classify items. This should be differentiated from the rounding procedure we applied before to large items, where we replace the original instance
I
with
\tilde{I}
.

Rounding weight vectors.

We round the scaled weight vectors of small items as follows.

1. Scaling:
For every item
j\in\hat{I}
, we define vector
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}^{l}_{j}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}/w^{l}_{j},
where
w^{l}_{j}=\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\|_{\infty}
. Consequently,
\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{l}_{j}\|_{\infty}=1
. In other word,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}^{l}_{j}
is the vector obtained by scaling up the largest coordinate of
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}
to 1, and then scale up other coordinates proportionally.

2. Rounding up the scaled vectors:
For every item
j
, we round up each coordinate of
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}^{l}_{j}
to the smallest value of the form
\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{h}
. Precisely, for
1\leq i\leq s_{B}
, if
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}^{l}_{j}[i]\leq\epsilon
, we round it up to
\epsilon
; otherwise it holds that
\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{h}<\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}^{l}_{j}[i]\leq\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{h+1}
for some
h\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\epsilon)
, we round it up to
\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{h+1}
. Denote by
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{l}_{j}
the rounded vector, we call it the
shape
of item
j
. Since every coordinate of
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{l}_{j}
can take at most
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\epsilon)
distinct values, thus there are at most
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\epsilon^{s_{B}})
distinct shapes.

3. Generating rounded weight vectors:
For every item
j
, we round up its weight weight to
w^{l}_{j}\cdot\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{l}_{j}
and denote by
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}
the rounded weight vector. It follows that
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\leq\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}
. Moreover, we have the following observation.

Observation 9
.

Given
(y_{1},\cdots,y_{\hat{n}})\in[0,1]^{\hat{n}}
satisfying
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
, we have
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq(1+(s_{B}+1)\epsilon)\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
.

Proof.

Given
(y_{1},\cdots,y_{\hat{n}})\in[0,1]^{\hat{n}}
satisfying
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
, the observation follows directly from the following two formulas:

\sum_{j:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}^{l}_{j}[i]\leq\epsilon}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}[i]{y}_{j}=\epsilon\sum_{j:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}^{l}_{j}[i]\leq\epsilon}w_{j}{y}_{j}\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{s_{B}}\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}[i]{y}_{j}\leq\epsilon s_{B},\hskip 5.69054pt1\leq i\leq s_{B};

\sum_{j:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}^{l}_{j}[i]>\epsilon}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}[i]{y}_{j}\leq(1+\epsilon)\sum_{j:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}^{l}_{j}[i]>\epsilon}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}[i]{y}_{j}\leq 1+\epsilon,\hskip 5.69054pt1\leq i\leq s_{B}.

∎

Item classification of
\hat{I}
.
We classify small items in the following way.

1. Classifying Shapes:
We first classify items according to their
shapes
. Precisely, we define the index sets
I^{l}_{h}
’s such that
j\in{I}^{l}_{h}
if and only if
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{l}_{j}
is the
h
-th rounded vector.

2. Classifying Ratios:
Define the
ratio
of each item
j
as
\rho^{l}_{j}=\tilde{p}_{j}/w^{l}_{j}
, where
w^{l}_{j}=\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\|_{\infty}
. Then in each
I^{l}_{h}
, we first divide items into three mega sub-groups: let
I^{(l,s)}_{h}
be the set of items whose ratio is smaller or equal to
\epsilon
; let
I^{(l,b)}_{h}
be the set of items whose ratio is larger than
1/\epsilon;
let
I^{(l,m)}_{h}
be the set of remaining items. Let
I^{(l,s)}=\mathop{\cup}\limits_{h}I^{(l,s)}_{h}
,
I^{(l,b)}=\mathop{\cup}\limits_{h}I^{(l,b)}_{h}
and
I^{(l,m)}=\mathop{\cup}\limits_{h}I^{(l,m)}_{h}
.

3. Sub-Classifying Medium Ratios:
In each
I^{(l,m)}_{h}
, we round down the ratio of the each item to the nearest value of the form
\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{k}
. More precisely, for item
j\in I^{(l,m)}_{h}
, it holds that
\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{k}\leq\rho^{l}_{j}<\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{k+1}
for some
k\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\epsilon)
, we round down the ratio
\rho^{l}_{j}
to
\epsilon(1+\epsilon)^{k}
.

After rounding, in each
I^{(l,m)}_{h}
, we divide items into subgroups, such that in each subgroup, items have the same shape and the same rounded ratio. These subgroups are denoted by
T^{(l,h)}_{1},T^{(l,h)}_{2},\dots,T^{(l,h)}_{\gamma_{(l,h)}}
.
Let
{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}_{k}^{(l,h)}
and
\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}
denote the shape and the rounded ratio of items in the same sub-group
T^{(l,h)}_{k}
, respectively. It holds that
\rho^{l}_{j}\leq\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}\leq(1+\epsilon)\rho^{l}_{j}
for
j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}
.

In conclude, we have divided items in
I^{(l,m)}
into at most
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\epsilon^{s_{B}+1})
sub-groups such that in each sub-group, items have the same shape and the same rounded ratio. This completes the preprocessing part. Again notice that we do not substitute item weights with rounded weights, and the instance is thus still
\hat{I}
.

Generating a well-structured follower’s solution.

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
be an optimal solution to
MBi-IP
{}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Given
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
, consider the following linear program:

\displaystyle\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{r^{*}}_{j},\quad\forall j\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 19.91692pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

In the following, we are going to generate a well-structured near-optimal solution to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
. According to the classification of items, items in
I^{(l,s)}
are the ones that the follower least want to select and items in
I^{(l,b)}
are the ones that the follower most want to select, whereas for the items in
I^{(l,m)}
, we are not clear about the preference of the follower, but we have subdivided items in
I^{(l,m)}
into subgroups so that we may approximately guess the follower’s choice on each subgroup. Based on this, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 14
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, then there exists a well-structured feasible solution to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
, say
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}=(\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1},\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2},\cdots,\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{\hat{n}})
, such that

•

\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=1-x_{j}^{r^{*}}
for
j\in I^{(l,b)}
;

•

\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=0
for
j\in I^{(l,s)}
;

•

for each
T^{(l,h)}_{k}
, either
\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=0
or
\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}(1+\epsilon)^{t}
for some
t\in\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(1/\epsilon).

Furthermore, let
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
be the optimal objective value of
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
, then the objective value of
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]=\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
is at least
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)
.

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
and
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
be an optimal solution and the optimal objective value of
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
, respectively.
Towards the proof of Lemma
14
,
we have the following observations.

Observation 10
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, then
P_{l}+OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
for any
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
.

Proof.

Recall that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
is an optimal solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and the optimal objective value of
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
is at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
. Note that
l
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
form a feasible solution to program (
11d
) - (
11g
) when
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
, it thus follows that
P_{l}+OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
.∎

Observation 11
.

For any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]\in[0,1]^{\hat{n}}
satisfying
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
, we have
\sum\limits_{j\in I^{(l,s)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}\leq{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)
.

Proof.

For any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]\in[0,1]^{\hat{n}}
satisfying
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}
, it suffices to observe that

\sum\limits_{j\in I^{(l,s)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}=\sum\limits_{j\in I^{(l,s)}}\rho^{l}_{j}w^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\epsilon\sum\limits_{j\in I^{(l,s)}}w^{l}_{j}y_{j},

\sum\limits_{j\in I^{(l,s)}}w^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{s_{B}}\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq s_{B}.

∎

Observation 12
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, then for any
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
, we have
\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon
. Furthermore, there exists a feasible solution
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}
to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
such that
\hat{y}_{j}=1-x_{j}^{r^{*}}
for
j\in I^{(l,b)}
.

Proof.

We first show that
\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon
. If
\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})>2s_{B}\epsilon
, then we can define a feasible solution
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]
to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
such that
y_{j}=\frac{2s_{B}\epsilon(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})}{\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})}
for
j\in I^{(l,b)}
, and
y_{j}=0
otherwise. It is easy to see that the objective value of
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
for this solution is greater than
2s_{B}
, contradicting the fact that
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
when
OPT\leq 1
. Hence,
\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon
.

For the second part of Observation
12
. Recall that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
is an optimal solution to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
. Define
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}[\hat{I}]\in[0,1]^{\hat{n}}
such that
\hat{y}_{j}=1-x_{j}^{r^{*}}
if
j\in I^{(l,b)}
; otherwise
\hat{y}_{j}=(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
. We claim that
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}[\hat{I}]
is a feasible solution to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
. It suffices to observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}

\displaystyle=(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})

\displaystyle\leq(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}+2s_{B}\epsilon\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}.

Hence Observation
12
is proved. ∎

Now we are ready to prove Lemma
14
.

Proof of Lemma
14
.

Recall that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
is an optimal solution to
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
. For each
T^{(l,h)}_{k}
, we define
L(T^{(l,h)}_{k})=\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
. Note that each item
j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}
satisfies
\rho^{l}_{j}\in(\epsilon,\frac{1}{\epsilon}]
. Since
\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}\rho^{l}_{j}w^{l}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
, we have
L(T^{(l,h)}_{k})\leq 2s_{B}/\epsilon
. If
L(T^{(l,h)}_{k})\leq\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}
, then
\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}=\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}\rho^{l}_{j}w^{l}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq\epsilon^{s_{B}+3}
. Hence, the overall contribution to profit from these groups is at most
{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
. If
L(T^{(l,h)}_{k})>\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}
, note that
L(T^{(l,h)}_{k})\leq 2s_{B}/\epsilon
, we may round down its value to the nearest value of the form
\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}(1+\epsilon)^{t}
for some
t\in\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(1/\epsilon)
and let it be
\tilde{L}(T^{(l,h)}_{k})
. Define
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\in[0,1]^{\hat{n}}
such that:

(i)

\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=0
if
j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}
and
L(T^{(l,h)}_{k})\leq\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}
;

(ii)

\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\theta^{(l,h)}_{k}y^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}
if
j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}
and
L(T^{(l,h)}_{k})>\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}
, where
\theta^{(l,h)}_{k}=\frac{\tilde{L}(T^{(l,h)}_{k})}{{L}(T^{(l,h)}_{k})}\in[1/(1+\epsilon),1]
;

(iii)

\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=1-x^{r^{*}}_{j}
if
j\in I^{(l,b)}
;

(iv)

\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=0
if
j\in I^{(l,s)}
.

We claim that
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
is a feasible solution to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
with an objective value of at least
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)
. The feasibility is guaranteed by

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})

\displaystyle\leq(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}+2s_{B}\epsilon\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}\geq\frac{1-2s_{B}\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-2s_{B}\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)\geq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon),

Hence Lemma
14
is proved. ∎

Finding a solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})

The goal of this section to construct a single level linear program whose extreme point optimal solution is a solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
.

Consider an optimal solution
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. For each
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
, we define
\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}
for every
T^{(l,h)}_{k}
as follows:

•

If
\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})\leq\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}
, then
\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}=0
;

•

If
\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})\geq 2s_{B}/\epsilon
, then
\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}=2s_{B}/\epsilon
;

•

If
\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}<\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})<2s_{B}/\epsilon
, then
\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}
is
\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})
rounded down to the nearest value of the form
\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}(1+\epsilon)^{t}
where
t\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\epsilon)
.

For each
T_{k}^{(l,h)}
,
\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}
may take
\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(1/\epsilon)
distinct values. Therefore given
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
, with
2^{\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(s_{B}/\epsilon^{2s_{B}+1})}
enumerations we can guess out
\{\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}:\forall l\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{s_{B}}{\epsilon^{s_{B}}});\forall h\in\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{s_{B}}});\forall k\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})\}
. Furthermore, for each well-structured solution
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
defined in Lemma
14
, we let
\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}
denote
\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w_{j}^{l}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}
, recall that
\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}
may take
\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(1/\epsilon)
distinct values, thus with
2^{\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(s_{B}/\epsilon^{2s_{B}+1})}
enumerations we can also guess out
\{\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}:\forall l\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{s_{B}}{\epsilon^{s_{B}}});\forall h\in\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{s_{B}}});\forall k\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})\}
. It is easy to see that
\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}\leq\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}
. Then we construct the following single level LP(given the guessed-out
\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}
’s and
\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}
’s ):

\displaystyle\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}:\quad\min\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},M}\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\tilde{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}_{j}x_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle P_{l}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}\leq M,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall l

\displaystyle\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}\leq\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j})\leq\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}(1+\epsilon),\hskip 5.69054pt\forall l,k,h\text{ such that }\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}\neq 0,2s_{B}/\epsilon

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j})\leq\epsilon^{s_{B}+4},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall l,k,h\text{ such that }\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}=0

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j})\geq 2s_{B}/\epsilon,\hskip 5.69054pt\forall l,k,h\text{ such that }\lambda^{(l,h)}_{k}=2s_{B}/\epsilon

\displaystyle x_{j}=x^{*}_{j},j\in\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}

\displaystyle x_{j}\in[0,1],j\in\hat{I}

Where
\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}
is the rounded ratio of items in
T^{(l,h)}_{k}
. Since the number of constraints in
cen-LP
λ
is a polynomial with respect to the number of variables, there exists polynomial time algorithm which returns an extreme point optimal solution to
cen-LP
λ
, denote by
\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda},M^{\lambda}\}
this solution. It is easy to see that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
is a feasible solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, furthermore, we have the following.

Lemma 15
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, let
\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda},M^{*}\}
be an extreme point optimal solution to
\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}
, then
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
is a feasible solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
.

The rest of this section is dedicated to proving Lemma
15
, which consists three main steps: (i)
given
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
and integer
l\in[1,\Theta]
, we first construct
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
. Then we construct another two LPs, i.e.,
\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
and
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
. By comparing the optimal objectives of these three LPs, showing that the optimal objective value of
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
is approximate to the optimal objective value of
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
; (ii) given
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
and integer
l\in[1,\Theta]
, we first construct
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
. Then we construct another two LPs, i.e.,
\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
and
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
. By comparing the optimal objectives of these three LPs, showing that with an additive error of
{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)
, the optimal objective value of
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
is less than
(1+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon))
times the optimal
objective value of
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
; (iii) we complete the proof of Lemma
15
by the results obtained in (i) and (ii).

Step 1

Recall that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
is an optimal solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. Given
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
and
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
, we consider the following LP:

\displaystyle\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{r^{*}}_{j},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 14.22636pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

Let
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
be the optimal objective value of
{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
. We have
P_{l}+OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq OPT^{MBi}_{r}
, where
OPT^{MBi}_{r}
is the optimal objective value of
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
.

Then we define the following two LPs:

\displaystyle\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,m)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}
