\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle y_{j}=0,\hskip 28.45274ptj\in I^{(l,s)}

\displaystyle y_{j}=1-x^{r^{*}}_{j},\hskip 5.69054ptj\in I^{(l,b)}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{r^{*}}_{j},\hskip 5.69054ptj\in I^{(l,m)}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 14.22636ptj\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,m)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j}),\hskip 5.69054pt\forall k,h

\displaystyle y_{j}=0,\hskip 28.45274ptj\in I^{(l,s)}

\displaystyle y_{j}=1-x^{r^{*}}_{j},\hskip 5.69054ptj\in I^{(l,b)}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 14.22636ptj\in I^{(l,m)}

Let
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}
and
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2}
be the optimal objective values of
\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
and
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
, respectively. Then we compare
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}
with
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
and
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2}
separately.

Step 1.1 Compare
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}
with
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
.

We have the following observation.

Observation 13
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, then we have

OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta).

Proof.

It follows directly from Lemma
14
and Observation
10
.
∎

Step 1.2 Compare
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}
with
OPT_{2}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
.

We have the following lemma.

Lemma 16
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, then we have

OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon).

Proof.

It is easy to see that
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2}
since any feasible solution to
\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r*})
is also a feasible solution to
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r*})
. In the next, we show that
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2}\leq(1+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon))OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}
. Then by the fact that
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
, we have
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon)
, which completes the proof.

Let
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}[\hat{I}]
be an optimal solution to
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r*})
, it suffices to show that there exists a feasible solution
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]
to
\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r*})
such that
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}\leq(1+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon))\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}
. Towards this, for each
h
and each
k
, we define the following LP (given
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}[\hat{I}]
):

\displaystyle\textbf{LP}^{(l,h)}_{k}:\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}y_{j}=\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x_{j}^{r*},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}

Since
\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}\leq\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r*})
, then one feasible solution to
LP
{}^{(l,h)}_{k}
can be obtained by a simple greedy algorithm that selects remaining
fractional items in
T_{k}^{(l,h)}
in the natural order of indices, until the budget
\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}
is exhausted.

Define
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{l}\in[0,1]^{|\hat{I}|}
such that for each
h
and each
k
,
\{y^{l}_{j}:j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}\}
is a feasible solution to
LP
{}^{(l,h)}_{k}
. Note that items in
T_{k}^{(l,h)}
have the same shape
{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}_{k}^{(l,h)}
, thus we have

\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}y^{l}_{j}\leq\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}y^{l}_{j}={{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}_{k}^{(l,h)}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}y^{l}_{j}={{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}_{k}^{(l,h)}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}=\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}.

It follows that

\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}y^{l}_{j}\leq\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}}^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}\leq(1+(s_{B}+1)\epsilon)\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},

where the last inequality holds by Observation
9
.

According to Observation
12
, we have
\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon
. Define
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]
such that: (i)
y_{j}=\frac{(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)y^{l}_{j}}{1+(s_{B}+1)\epsilon}
if
j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}
; (ii)
y_{j}=1-x^{r*}_{j}
if
j\in I^{(l,b)}
; (iii)
y_{j}=0
if
j\in I^{(l,s)}
. We claim that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]
is a feasible solution to
\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;x^{r*})
. It suffices to observe that

\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}=\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}+(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}\rho^{l}_{j}w^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}\leq(1+\epsilon)\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}

\displaystyle=(1+\epsilon)\frac{1+(s_{B}+1)\epsilon}{1-2s_{B}\epsilon}\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}w^{l}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)\frac{1+(s_{B}+1)\epsilon}{1-2s_{B}\epsilon}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}.

It follows that

\sum\limits_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}_{j}\leq\sum\limits_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}{y}_{j}+(1+\epsilon)\frac{1+(1+s_{B})\epsilon}{1-2s_{B}\epsilon}\sum\limits_{h}\sum\limits_{k}\sum\limits_{j\in T_{k}^{(l,h)}}\tilde{p}_{j}{y}_{j}\leq(1+{\mathcal{O}}(s^{2}_{B}\epsilon))\sum\limits_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}.

∎

Besides, we have the following observation.

Observation 14
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, then for any
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
, we have

OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)\leq\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}.

Moreover, we have

P_{l}+\max\{OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1},OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2}\}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon).

Proof.

Given
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
an optimal solution to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
, let
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
be the well-structured feasible solution to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
defined in Lemma
14
, then we have

\displaystyle OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}\rho^{l}_{j}w^{l}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}\left(\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}(OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)).

According to Observation
10
,
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
, it thus follows that

OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)\leq\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}.

For the second part of the observation, notice that
\max\{OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1},OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2}\}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon)
and
P_{l}+OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
, thus

P_{l}+\max\{OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{1},OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{2}\}\leq P_{l}+OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon)\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon).

∎

Step 2

Recall that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
is an optimal basic solution of
\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}
. Given
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
and any
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
, we consider the following LP:

\displaystyle\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}[i]y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{\lambda}_{j},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 11.38109pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

Let
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}
be the optimal objective value of
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
. Let
Obj^{MBi}_{r}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
be the objective value of
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
, we have
P_{l}+OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}\leq Obj^{MBi}_{r}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
.

Then we define the following two LPs:

\displaystyle\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{\lambda})+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,m)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle y_{j}=0,\hskip 25.60747ptj\in I^{(l,s)}

\displaystyle y_{j}=1-x^{\lambda}_{j},\hskip 5.69054ptj\in I^{(l,b)}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{\lambda}_{j},\hskip 5.69054ptj\in I^{(l,m)}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 14.22636ptj\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{\lambda})+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,m)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\sum_{j\in T^{(l,h)}_{k}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j}),\hskip 5.69054pt\forall k,h

\displaystyle y_{j}=0,\hskip 28.45274ptj\in I^{(l,s)}

\displaystyle y_{j}=1-x^{\lambda}_{j},\hskip 8.53581ptj\in I^{(l,b)}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 17.07164ptj\in I^{(l,m)}

Let
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{1}
and
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{2}
be the optimal objective values of
\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
and
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
, respectively. Then we compare
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{1}
with
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}
and
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{2}
separately.

Step 2.1 Compare
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{1}
with
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}

We claim that
(1-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon))OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{1}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}
. It follows directly from the following lemma.

Lemma 17
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, then there exists a well-structured solution
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}
to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
such that

•

\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}=1-x_{j}^{\lambda}
for
j\in I^{(l,b)}
;

•

\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{j}=0
for
j\in I^{(l,s)}
.

Furthermore, the objective value of

LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]=\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}
is at least
(1-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon))OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)
.

Towards the proof of Lemma
17
,
we first give the following observations.

Observation 15
.

Let
M^{r^{*}}=\max\{P_{l}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}:\forall l\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,|\Theta|]\}
. Then
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
and
M^{r^{*}}
form a feasible solution to
cen-LP
λ
. Furthermore, if
OPT\leq 1
, then
M^{r^{*}}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
.

Proof.

The first part of the observation is easy to see. Consider the second part of the observation. Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}
be an optimal solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, we assume that
M^{r^{*}}=P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}
for some
l^{\prime}\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
. Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
be an optimal solution of the following linear program:

\displaystyle\textbf{LP}(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}}):\quad\max\limits_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l^{\prime}}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{r^{*}}_{j},\forall j\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\forall j\in\hat{I}

It is easy to see that
P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq OPT^{MBi}_{r}
. According to Lemma
14
, there exists a well-structured feasible solution to
LP
(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})
, say
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}
, such that

\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\sum_{j\in T^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}}\rho^{l^{\prime}}_{j}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}
and
\sum_{j\in T^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}=\bar{\lambda}^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}
. Since
\rho^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\leq\rho^{l^{\prime}}_{j}
for
j\in T^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}
, we have

P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{r^{*}}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\leq P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}\tilde{y}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}\leq P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}_{j}.

Then the observation follows by the fact that
OPT^{MBi}_{r}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
if
OPT\leq 1
(Lemma
13
).
∎

Observation 16
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, then
\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon
for each
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
.

Proof.

Let
\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda},M^{\lambda}\}
be an optimal solution to
cen-LP
λ
. For each
l\in\{1,2,\cdots,|\Theta|\}
, it holds that

P_{l}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l,h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}^{(l,h)}_{k}\leq M^{\lambda}\leq M^{r^{*}}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),

where the last inequality holds by Observation
15
. Recall that
\rho^{l}_{j}>{1}/{\epsilon}
for
j\in I^{(l,b)}
, thus we have

\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})\leq\epsilon\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})\leq\epsilon(s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon.

∎

Now we are ready to prove Lemma
17
.

Proof of Lemma
17
.

Since
OPT\leq 1
, we have
\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon
by Observation
16
. Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}
be an optimal solution of
\textbf{LP}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
, define
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}\in[0,1]^{\hat{n}}
such that:

(i)

\bar{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{j}=1-x_{j}^{\lambda}
if
j\in I^{(l,b)}
;

(ii)

\bar{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{j}=0
if
j\in I^{(l,s)}
;

(iii)

\bar{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{j}=(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}
if
j\in I^{(l,m)}
.

We claim that
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}
is a feasible solution to
LP
(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
with an objective value of at least
(1-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon))\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)
. The feasibility is guaranteed by

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}\bar{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{\lambda})+(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}w^{l}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{\lambda})\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}+(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l,b)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}^{(l,\lambda)}

\displaystyle\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}+(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}.

On the other hand,
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}\bar{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\bar{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{j}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,m)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\bar{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{j}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l,s)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\bar{y}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{j}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}+(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l,b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)

\displaystyle\geq(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}^{(l,\lambda)}-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon).

Where
{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)
comes from Observation
11
. Hence, Lemma
17
is proved.
∎

Step 2.2 Compare
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{1}
with
OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{2}

We have the following observation.

Observation 17
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, then we have

OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{1}\leq OPT^{(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}_{2}
.

Proof.

It follows directly from the fact that any feasible solution to
\overline{\textbf{LP}}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
is also a feasible solution to
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l;{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
.
∎

Step 3

Recall that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
is an extreme point optimal solution to
cen-LP
λ
. The objective value of
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
is the optimal objective value of the following program:

\displaystyle\textbf{Lower-LP:}\quad\max_{1\leq l\leq|\Theta|}\max_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]}\quad

\displaystyle P_{\ell}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 8.53581pt

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l}_{j}y_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}

\displaystyle y_{j}\leq 1-x^{\lambda}_{j},\hskip 5.69054pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

\displaystyle y_{j}\in[0,1],\hskip 11.38109pt\forall j\in\hat{I}

Assume that
l^{\prime}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime}[\hat{I}]
together form an optimal solution to
Lower-LP
. If
OPT\leq 1
, according to the previous discussions, we have

(1-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon))(P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}^{\prime})-{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\epsilon)\leq P_{l^{\prime}}+OPT_{1}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}\leq P_{l^{\prime}}+OPT_{2}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}.

If we can prove that the
P_{l^{\prime}}+OPT_{2}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
, then we have
P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y^{\prime}_{j}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s^{2}_{B}\epsilon)
, which completes the proof of Lemma
15
. From now on, we aim to prove the following lemma.

Lemma 18
.

If
OPT\leq 1
, assuming that
l^{\prime}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime}[\hat{I}]
form an optimal solution to
Lower-LP
, then
P_{l^{\prime}}+OPT_{2}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
.

Proof.

Let
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}[\hat{I}]
be an optimal solution to
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
.

When
\lambda^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\neq 0
and
\lambda^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\neq\frac{2s_{B}}{\epsilon}
, recall that both
\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r*},M^{r^{*}}\}
and
\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda},M^{\lambda}\}
are feasible solutions to
\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}
, we have

\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}\leq\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})\leq(1+\epsilon)\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}(1-x^{r*}_{j}).

When
\lambda_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}=2s_{B}/\epsilon
, we have
\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}(1-x^{r*}_{j})\geq 2s_{B}/\epsilon\geq 1
. Note that items in
T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}
have the same shape, thus there exists
i(1\leq i\leq s_{B})
such that

\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}=\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l^{\prime}}_{j}[i]\hat{y}_{j}\leq 1\leq\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}(1-x^{r*}_{j}).

When
\lambda_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}=0
, we have

\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}\leq\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})\leq\epsilon^{s_{B}+4}.

Define
H^{l^{\prime}}=\{(h,k)|\lambda_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}=0,\forall k,h\}
. There are at most
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\epsilon^{s_{B}+1})
elements in
H^{l^{\prime}}
.
The overall contribution to profit from items in
\cup_{(k,h)\in H^{l^{\prime}}}T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}
is

\displaystyle\sum_{(h,k)\in H^{l^{\prime}}}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(h,k)\in H^{l^{\prime}}}(1+\epsilon)\rho^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)/\epsilon\sum_{(h,k)\in H^{l^{\prime}}}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}\leq{{\mathcal{O}}}(\epsilon).

According to Observation
12
, we have
\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon
. Define
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]\in[0,1]^{\hat{n}}
such that:

(i)

y_{j}=\frac{1-2s_{B}\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\hat{y}_{j}
if
j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}
and
\lambda_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}\neq 0
;

(ii)

y_{j}=0
if
j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}
and
\lambda_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}=0
;

(iii)

y_{j}=1-x^{r*}_{j}
if
j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}
;

(iv)

y_{j}=0
if
j\in I^{(l^{\prime},s)}
.

We claim that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}[\hat{I}]
is a feasible solution to
\textbf{LP}^{\prime}({l^{\prime}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r*})
. It suffices to observe that

•

\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}y_{j}\leq\frac{1-2s_{B}\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}\leq(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}(1-x^{r*}_{j})\leq\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}(1-x^{r*}_{j}),\hskip 5.69054pt\forall k,h;

•

\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l^{\prime}}_{j}y_{j}\leq\left(\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}w^{l^{\prime}}_{j}(1-x^{r*}_{j})\right)\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},m)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l^{\prime}}_{j}y_{j}\leq 2s_{B}\epsilon\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}+(1-2s_{B}\epsilon)\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},m)}}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}^{l^{\prime}}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}.

According to Observation
14
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\hat{I}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})+\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}\leq OPT_{2}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}\leq OPT^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{r^{*}})}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x_{j}^{r^{*}})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon).

It thus follows that
\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}\leq\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon)
. Note that
M^{r^{*}}\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}\delta)
by Observation
15
, then we have

\displaystyle P_{l^{\prime}}+\frac{1-2s_{B}\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\left(\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})+\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},m)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})+\frac{1-2s_{B}\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},m)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})+\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}+\sum_{(h,k)\in H^{l^{\prime}}}\sum_{j\in T_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}}\tilde{p}_{j}\hat{y}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})+\sum_{j\not\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}y_{j}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq P_{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\in I^{(l^{\prime},b)}}\tilde{p}_{j}(1-x^{\lambda}_{j})+\sum_{h}\sum_{k}\rho^{(l^{\prime},h)}_{k}\bar{\lambda}_{k}^{(l^{\prime},h)}+{\mathcal{O}}(s^{2}_{B}\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq M^{\lambda}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon)\leq M^{r^{*}}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon)\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(s_{B}^{2}\epsilon).

Thus
P_{l^{\prime}}+OPT_{2}^{(l^{\prime};{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})}\leq s_{B}+{{\mathcal{O}}}(s_{B}^{3}\epsilon)
, and Lemma
18
is proved.
∎

A.3.2
Rounding the fractional solution

Recall that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
is an extreme point optimal solution to
cen-LP
λ
and is also a feasible solution to
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
. We round down
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
to
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda}
such that
\tilde{x}^{\lambda}_{j}=0
if
x^{\lambda}_{j}
is a fractional number, otherwise
\tilde{x}^{\lambda}_{j}=x^{\lambda}_{j}
. Note that both the construction of
\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}
and the ellipsoid method to solve
\textbf{cen-LP}_{\lambda}
run in polynomial time, i.e., the above algorithm we have designed to return
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda}
runs in polynomial time. We still need to prove that
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda}
is a feasible solution to
\textbf{IPC}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
.

Note that in
\{x^{\lambda}_{j}:j\in\hat{I}\}
, there are at most
s_{A}+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{s_{B}}{\epsilon^{2s_{B}+1}})
variables take fractional values. Since
s_{A}
and
s_{B}
are fixed constants,
\epsilon_{>0}
could be extremely small such that
s_{A}+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{s_{B}}{\epsilon^{2s_{B}+1}})\leq 1/\epsilon^{2s_{B}+3}
. Let
\delta=\epsilon^{2s_{B}+4}
, then we have the following.

Lemma 19
.

Let
\widetilde{Obj}_{2\delta}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})
be the objective value of
\textbf{IPC}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda}
. Let
Obj^{MBi}_{r}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
be the objective value of
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
. We have

\widetilde{Obj}_{2\delta}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})\leq Obj^{MBi}_{r}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon).

Proof.

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
be an extreme point optimal solution to
cen-LP
λ
, we define
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda}
such that
\tilde{x}^{\lambda}_{j}=0
if
x^{\lambda}_{j}
is a fractional number and
\tilde{x}^{\lambda}_{j}=x^{\lambda}_{j}
otherwise. Let
Obj^{MBi}_{r}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})
be an objective value of
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda}
. Let
\widetilde{Obj}_{2\delta}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})
,
Obj^{MBi}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})
and
Obj^{MBi}_{r}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})
be the objective values of
\textbf{IPC}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
,
\textbf{MBi-IP}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
and
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda}
, respectively.

Recall that
\delta=\epsilon^{2s_{B}+4}
and
\tilde{p}_{j}\leq\delta
for
j\in\hat{I}
. Since
\sum_{j\in\hat{I}:x^{\lambda}_{j}\in(0,1)}1\leq 1/s^{2s_{B}+3}
and
\tilde{x}^{\lambda}_{j}=x^{*}_{j}=x^{\lambda}_{j}
for
j\in\tilde{I}\setminus\hat{I}
, thus we have

Obj^{MBi}_{r}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})\leq Obj^{MBi}_{r}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})+\sum\limits_{j\in\hat{I}:x^{\lambda}_{j}\in(0,1)}\tilde{p}_{j}\leq Obj^{MBi}_{r}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})+\epsilon.

According to Lemma
15
, we have
Obj^{MBi}_{r}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\lambda})\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
, thus
Obj^{MBi}_{r}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})\leq s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
.

On the other hand, according to Lemma
12
, we have
\widetilde{Obj}_{2\delta}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})\leq Obj^{MBi}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})+\epsilon
. And it holds that
Obj^{MBi}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})\leq Obj^{MBi}_{r}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{\lambda})
by the fact that in
\textbf{MBi-IP}_{r}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
, the leader is facing a stronger follower who can fractionally pack items. Hence the Lemma
19
is proved.∎

In conclude, we have designed a polynomial time algorithm that returns a feasible solution to
\textbf{IPC}_{2\delta}(\tilde{I},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}})
with an objective value of at most
s_{B}+{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)
. According to Lemma
8
, we have proved Lemma
7
.

Appendix B
Omitted contents in Section
5

Theorem
4
.

Given a separation oracle
O_{L}
for the leader’s problem, and an oracle
O_{F}
for the follower’s problem that returns a
\rho
-approximation solution, there exists a
(\frac{\rho}{1-\alpha},\frac{1}{\alpha})
-bicriteria approximation algorithm for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
that returns a solution
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
such that
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}
, and

\max\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}\}\leq\frac{\rho T^{*}}{1-\alpha},

where
T^{*}
is the optimal objective value of
IPC
(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
. Furthermore, the algorithm runs in polynomial oracle time.

Towards the proof of Theorem
4
, we start with an equivalent formulation of
IPC
(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
as follows:

\displaystyle\textbf{IPC}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}):\min\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle s.t.\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x_{j})y_{j}\leq T,\ \forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\Omega:=\{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\}

Note that
IPC
{}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
is an IP with possibly exponential number of constraints. Replacing
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
with
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in[0,1]^{n}
, we obtain a linear relaxation and denote it as
IPC
{}_{f}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
.

We start with a weaker result.

Lemma 20
.

Given a separation oracle
O_{L}
for the leader’s problem, and an oracle
O_{F}
for the follower’s problem that always returns the optimal solution,
IPC
{}_{f}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
can be solved in polynomial oracle time.

Proof.

Note that
IPC
{}_{f}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
is equivalent to the following determine problem: given an arbitrary fixed target value
T_{0}
, does there exist
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in[0,1]^{n}
such that
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 1$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 1$}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq T_{0}
for all
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\Omega
? The determine problem can be formulated as the following linear program:

\displaystyle\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0}):
