\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in[0,1]^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x_{j})y_{j}\leq T_{0},\ \forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\Omega:=\{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\}

In the following we test the feasibility of
\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0})
through ellipsoid method. We give a very brief description of ellipsoid method and the reader may refer to, e.g.,
[
20
]
for details.

The ellipsoid method iteratively computes a sequence of ellipsoids
E_{0},E_{1},\cdots
. In each iteration, it implements a separation oracle to check whether the center of the current ellipsoid
E_{k}
is feasible. If it is, then it terminates and returns this point; Otherwise, it finds out a violating constraint as a cut. Incorporating the cut output by the separation oracle, the ellipsoid method computes a new ellipsoid
E_{k+1}
and guarantees that the volume of the new ellipsoid is smaller than
E_{k}
by a significant factor. After a polynomial number of iterations, the ellipsoid method either returns a feasible solution, or asserts that there is no feasible solution and returns a polynomial number of cuts as a certificate.

Towards solving
\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0})
, it suffices to design a separation oracle such that given any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}\in[0,1]^{n}
, it either asserts that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
is feasible to
\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0})
, or returns a constraint of
\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0})
that is violated by
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
.

We construct the separation oracle
SEP
as follows. Consider the following IP:

\displaystyle\textbf{IP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}):\max\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x^{0}_{j})y_{j}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

Note that
IP
{}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})
is the follower’s problem with item profits being
p_{j}(1-x^{0}_{j})
for
j=1,\cdots,n
. Apply the oracle
O_{F}
for the follower’s problem, we get an optimal solution to
\textbf{IP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})
and denote it as
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*}
.
If the optimal objective value of
\textbf{IP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})
is larger than
T_{0}
, then we know
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
violates the constraint
\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x_{j})y_{j}^{*}\leq T_{0}
and
SEP
returns this violating constraint. Otherwise, we apply the leader’s separation oracle
O_{L}
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
. If
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
does not satisfy
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}
,
O_{L}
returns a violating constraint, and we let
SEP
return the same constraint. Otherwise,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
is feasible to
\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0})
.
∎

Generalizing our technique for Lemma
20
, we obtain the following strengthened lemma.

Lemma 21
.

Given a separation oracle
O_{L}
for the leader’s problem, and an oracle
O_{F}
for the follower’s problem that returns a
\rho
-approximation solution, a
\rho
-approximation solution to
IPC
{}_{f}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
can be found in polynomial oracle time.

Proof.

Again, for any target value
T_{0}
, we consider the following:

\displaystyle\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0}):

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in[0,1]^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x_{j})y_{j}\leq T_{0},\ \forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\Omega:=\{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\}

We first present an ellipsoid method based algorithm
Al
and then show its property.

Al
works as follows. Consider the following IP:

\displaystyle\textbf{IP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}):\max\hskip 2.84526pt

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x^{0}_{j})y_{j}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf B$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

Apply the oracle for the follower’s problem, we get a
\rho
-approximation solution to
\textbf{IP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})
and denote it as
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{0}
. If
\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x^{0}_{j})y_{j}^{0}\leq T_{0}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
satisfies that
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}
,
Al
stops and returns
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
. Otherwise, either
\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x^{0}_{j})y_{j}^{0}>T_{0}
, then
Al
returns the violating constraint
\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x_{j})y_{j}^{0}\leq T_{0}
to
\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0})
and continues; or
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
does not satisfy that
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}
, then
Al
returns the same violating constraint that is returned by
O_{L}
and continues.

Now we analyze
Al
. We know the ellipsoid method based algorithm
Al
either returns some
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
at some iteration and stops, or after a polynomial number of iterations it ends up with an empty volume ellipsoid and terminates without finding any feasible solution.

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*}
be an optimal solution to
\textbf{IP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})
and let
T^{*}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})=\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x^{0}_{j})y_{j}^{*}
. We have the following observations.

Observation 18
.

If
T^{*}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})>\rho T_{0}
, then
Al
must return a violating constraint
\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x_{j})y_{j}^{0}\leq T_{0}
.

The observation follows as
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{0}
is a
\rho
-approximation solution, and thus
\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x^{0}_{j})y_{j}^{0}\geq T^{*}(x^{0})/\rho>T_{0}
.

Observation 19
.

If
T^{*}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})\leq T_{0}
and
A{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}
, then
Al
must return
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
.

The observation follows as the feasible solution
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{0}
cannot have an objective value larger than that of the optimal solution.

If, however,
T^{*}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})\in(T_{0},\rho T_{0}]
,
Al
either returns some
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
or terminates without finding any solution. Consequently, if
Al
returns some
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
, then
T^{*}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0})\leq\rho T_{0}
; if
Al
terminates without finding any solution, then
Al
has found a polynomial number of violating constraints
\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x_{j})y_{j}^{k}\leq T_{0}
for
k=0,1,\cdots,m
, together with a polynomial number of constraints from
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf A$}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}
, such that there does not exist
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in[0,1]^{n}
satisfying these constraints simultaneously.
That is,
Al
returns a polynomial number of constraints a certificate that
\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0})
does not admit any feasible solution. Hence, the following is true.

Claim 1
.

Al
either returns some solution
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{0}
which guarantees that
\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x^{0}_{j})y_{j}\leq\rho T_{0}
for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\Omega
; or it asserts that there does not exist any feasible solution to
\textbf{LP}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}(T_{0})
, and hence the optimal objective value of
IPC
{}_{f}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
is larger than
T_{0}
.

Hence, with binary search, we obtain a
\rho
-approximation solution for
IPC
{}_{f}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
. ∎

Finally, we are able to obtain a bicriteria-approximation solution to
IPC
{}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
by rounding the
\rho
-approximation solution to
IPC
{}_{f}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
, thus finalizing the proof of Theorem
4
.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Denote by
T^{*}_{f}
and
T^{*}
the optimal objective values of
IPC
{}_{f}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
and
IPC
{}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
, respectively. Then it follows that
T^{*}_{f}\leq T^{*}
.
Let
\textstyle\bf x
be a
\rho
-approximation solution to
IPC
{}_{f}^{1}(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
. Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
be any fixed parameter. We round up
x_{j}
to 1 if
x_{j}\geq\alpha
, otherwise, round down
x_{j}
to 0. Denote by
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
this integral solution, we have: (i)
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
violates the leader’s budget by a factor of
\frac{1}{\alpha}
since
A(\alpha\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})\leq A{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}}
; (ii)
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-\hat{x}_{j})y_{j}=\sum\limits_{j:x_{j}<\alpha}p_{j}y_{j}+\sum\limits_{j:x_{j}\geq\alpha}0\leq\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}p_{j}(1-x_{j})y_{j}\leq\frac{\rho}{1-\alpha}T^{*}_{f}\leq\frac{\rho}{1-\alpha}T^{*}
. Thus
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
is a
(\frac{\rho}{1-\alpha},\frac{1}{\alpha})
-bicriteria approximation solution of
IPC
(I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf b$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf b$}}})
. Theorem
4
is proved. ∎
